                     PREFACE

  Realistic combat training (RCT) enhances individual performance and
improves unit evaluations. It hones combat readiness and provides
interesting and challenging training. RCT improves the results of the skill
qualification tests (SQT) and the Army training and evaluation program
(ARTEP).

  This manual, prepared as part of the US Army’s Opposing Force
(OPFOR) Program (AR 350-2), is designed for use by commanders, their
staffs, and training managers. It is not an intelligence document. It is a
training reference that identifies the North Korean nation as a potential
adversary of the United States. In describing this potential adversary,
information is provided on the nation and its military forces. This informa-
tion also includes tactical doctrine, organization, weapons and equipment,
and a notional order of battle (OB) suitable for unclassified simulations
and exercises. This manual, therefore, provides information for combat
preparedness through threat training applications.

  Users of this manual are encouraged to submit recommendations to
improve its clarity or accuracy. Comments should be keyed to the specific
page, paragraph, and line of the text in which the change is recommended.
Reasons or references should be provided for each comment to insure
understanding and complete evaluation. Comments should be prepared,
using DA Form 2028 (Recommended Changes to Publications), and
forwarded directly to the Commander, US Army Intelligence Center and
School, ATTN: ATSI-TD-SPO, Fort Huachuca, AZ 85613.

  The words “he,” “him,” “his,” and “men,” when used in this manual
represent both the masculine and feminine genders unless specifically
stated.

                                   2 TWO-SIDED AMBUSH

THIS TACTIC IS EMPLOYED ON ROADS FREQUENTLY USED BY TROOPS. THE

CONSIDERED THE BEST METHOD FOR COMPLETE DESTRUCTION OR CAPTURE OF
PERSONNEL AND EQUIPMENT.

12-4. Company and Battalion Designations

  All company- and battalion-sized units within the .NKPA are numbered 1
through 9. A separate regiment or like unit has only battalions 1 through 3.
Examples are:

12-7. CODENUMBERS

  Code numbers are permanently assigned to each unit in the military establishment, but
may be changed for the duration of a given operation. The NKPA, for security reasons,
often refers to specific units by their code number. Code numbers for all units appear to be
assigned in a completely arbitrary manner. A listing of the notional code numbers with a
reference to the paragraph is provided below.

FM 34-71

          HEAVY PONTON BRIDGE, TRP (HALF SET)

                           Construction and basic employment concept are
                           similar to those of the light ponton bridge, LPP.
                           One bridge section consists of a bow section and
                           center section, each carried on a single truck.

                           Employment in rivers with current velocity
                           under 9 FPS is by raft. With velocity over 9 FPS,
                           assembly is by successive pontons.

                           Successful employment of the TPP in currents
                           of 13 FPS has been reported.

          CHARACTERISTICS  16T      50T        70T

          Length           163m      135m       103m
          Width, Roadway    3.2m     4.0m       4.0m
          Assembly Time    70 min   60 min     70 min
          Maximum Current  8 FPS    8 FPS      8 FPS

          Remarks: Ferries of 50 tons and 70 tons can also be constructed. Assembly times are 50
          to 100 percent longer at night. BMK-90 powerboats are used with the TPP bridge.

          HEAVY PONTON BRIDGE, TPP

             PKhR CHEMICAL AGENT DETECTION AND IDENTIFICATION KIT

                                             The PKhR Chemical Agent Detection and
                                             Identification kit can identify the following US
                                             agents:

                                             Agent                US Agent Symbol

                                             Mustard                     H or HD
                                             Nitrogen mustard                HN
                                             Lewisite                         L
                                             Hydrogen cyanide               AC
                                             Chloropicrin                    PS

                                                 FM 34-71

    Appendix A

INDICATORS

General                                            The following paragraphs contain intelli-
                                                 gence indicators and an explanation for each.
  In spite of all precautions to deceive, North  The listing is not complete and is not
Korea must inevitably carry out specific         intended for dogmatic application in all
activities in preparation for or in conjunction  situations. It is primarily a sampling of
with specific actions. These activities may be   typical North Korean activities.
detected, evaluated, and intérpreted to
develop a reasonable estimate of North
Korea’s probable courses of action. However,
North Korea is probably aware of these esti-
mates and may attempt to turn apparent
vulnerability into deceptive measures.

Attack                                                          EXPLANATION

                   ACTIVITY                      Areas of secondary importance are often
Massing of infantry, armor, and artillery.       denuded to mass maximum strength for the
                                                 main effort.
Deployment of combat elements, such
as infantry and armor, in echelon.               Normal attack formation provides for
                                                 the second echelon of the regiment to be
Forward elements disposed on a relatively        located 3 to 5 kilometers in the rear of
narrow front. Concentration of mass              the first echelon, division second echelon
toward either or both flanks.                    5 to 7 kilometers in the rear of the first eche-
Tanks dispersed to front echelons.               lon and corps echelon 10 to 20 kilometers in
Extensive artillery preparation.                 the rear of the first echelon.

Artillery positions well forward and             The attack zone of an infantry regiment
concentrated.                                    attempted during the offense. Tanks
Air defense weapons located in forward           and infantry on either or both flanks
areas.                                           may indicate a single or double envelopment.

                                                 Tanks leading assault elements of infantry.

                                                 Offensive actions are built around
                                                 the striking power and shock of massed
                                                 artillery. Preparations normally precede the
                                                 offense by 1/2 to 1 hour.

                                                 Artillery positions are well forward with
                                                 concentrated direct fire weapons, artillery
                                                 pieces, and large numbers of mortars.

                                                 Air defense assets displaced forward
                                                 before an attack to protect assault forces
                                                 and facilitate forward displacement during
                                                 the attack.

                                                                                                       A-1


---

FM 34-71

Attack (Continued)                                             EXPLANATION

                    ACTIVITY                   Lanes are cleared and marked before
Clearing lanes through obstacles within        the attack to facilitate forward movement
own positions.                                 and grouping, particularly at night.

Reconnaissance and destruction of obstacles    Usually on night preceding attack, NKPA
that are a part of friendly defenses.          patrols reconnoiter friendly obstacles
                                               to determine a plan for clearing lanes. Patrols
Demonstrations and feints.                     will only destroy those obstacles that will not
                                               disclose the direction of the main attack.
Increased patrolling.
                                               Local, small-scale attacks involving infan-
Increased air reconnaissance.                  try, tanks, and artillery frequently precede a
                                               general attack.
Movement of hostile forces forward and
location of NKPA troops in forward             Patrols by infantry and mechanized units
assembly areas.                                will normally increase before an attack.
Increased activity in rear areas.
                                               Air reconnaissance will normally become
Systematic air attack.                         more active before a general attack.

Conducting drills and rehearsals in            Before launching an attack, troops
rear areas.                                    are deployed forward into assembly areas
                                               from which they can launch the attack.

                                               Supply and administrative activities in-
                                               crease in the rear areas before the attack.

                                               Before the attack, the enemy may engage
                                               in systematic air attacks and bombardment
                                               to soften friendly positions.

                                               Major attacks may be preceded by rehearsals.
                                               This is particularly true of attacks against
                                               fortified positions and heavily defended river
                                               lines.

Defense                                                       EXPLANATION
                   ACTIVITY
                                               Defense is based on a stubborn defense
Preparation of battalion and company           of battalion areas and counterattacks
defensive areas.                               by tank-heavy forces.

Extensive preparation of field fortified       The NKPA makes extensive use of trenches,
positions.                                     prepared positions, and overhead cover in
                                               defensive operations.
Large tank units located in assembly
areas to the rear.                             Tank units are held in the rear to be
Preparation of alternate artillery positions.  employed in counterattack roles.

A-2                                            In normal defensive operations, three
                                               positions are prepared for each firing battery.


---

Defense (Continued)                                                                  FM 34-71

                  ACTIVITY                                 EXPLANATION
Preparation of successive defense lines.    In the defense, separate and distinct
Entrenching and erecting bands of wire.     defense lines are prepared.
                                            Digging of trenches and the erection of
Presence of demolitions, contaminated       wire indicate preparations to hold the
areas, obstacles, and minefields.           position.
                                            These emplacements and devices are
                                            used to cover avenues of approach into
                                            NKA positions.

Delay                                                      EXPLANATION

                   ACTIVITY                 In a delay, units will avoid becoming deci-
Withdrawal from defensive positions before  sively engaged.
becoming heavily engaged.
Successive local counterattacks with        Counterattacks are employed to assist
limited objectives.                         first echelon units in disengaging rather
                                            than to restore positions.
Counterattacks broken off before position
is restored.                                Same as above.
Use of pre-positioned nuclear weapons.
                                            Pre-positioned nuclear weapons facilitate
Frontages up to four times that normally    the delay.
assigned to units on the defensive.
Maximum firepower positioned forward;       Forces conducting delays are normally
firing initiated at long ranges.            assigned extremely wide frontages.

                                            Long-range engagement facilitates the
                                            the delay.

Reinforcement                                              EXPLANATION
                                            This action will increase the NKPA’s
                   ACTIVITY                 present strength.
Movement of additional troops toward
the front.                                  A major increase in traffic toward
Increased traffic toward the front.         the front may indicate more than normal
                                            resupply activity and additional troops.
Identification of new units in combat
zone.                                       New units may increase the NKPA’s
Additional command posts.                   present strength.

                                            Presence of additional units will cause
                                            an increase in these installations.

                                                                                            A-3

FM 34-71

Nuclear Weapons              FROG  units                  EXPLANATION

                   ACTIVITY                Missile and FROG units are located
Location of missile or                     within one-third maximum range on the
within striking range.                     offense and one-half on the defense.

Use of missiles or FROGs with HE           May indicate supporting fires or may
warheads.                                  use for registration purposes.

Special or unusual activity by front-line  Frontline troops may construct unusually
troops.                                    deep or covered foxholes before using .
                                           a nuclear weapon.
Limited withdrawal of front-line units
without apparent tactical reason.          Frontline units may withdraw to avoid
                                           casualty-producing close-in nuclear
Sudden and energetic digging in.           weapons.

Use of smoke cover on front-line troops    Frontline troops may be ordered to
without apparent tactical reason.          take immediate protective measures before
                                           using a nuclear weapon.
Sudden increase in communications and
electronic activity.                       The haze may be used to protect
                                           troops from the thermal affects of
Disappearance of known enemy agents        detonation.
from specific areas.
                                           Last-minute orders, warnings, and the
Increased or unusual air activity.         use of meteorological radars may
                                           indicate the preparation for use of nuclear
                                           weapons.

                                           Before a nuclear attack, agents may be
                                           ordered to leave the area.

                                           Aerially delivered nuclear weapons may
                                           require a degree of local air superiority,
                                           photo missions, or practice flight runs
                                           by delivery aircraft.
